      "url": "https://www.heatheronhertravels.com/food-in-munich-where-and-what-to-eat",
      "title": "Delicious Food in Munich Germany - what and where to eat",
      "content": "We enjoyed our al fresco lunch here on the Tambosi restaurant terrace, watching the groups playing boules and the cyclists passing by. The menu was Italian inspired with pizza and pasta, as well as colourful salads served with fresh bread and beer. Prices reflected the prime location, but it was a very pretty and stylish place for lunch on a fine summer’s day.\n\n> More info: Tambosi Website | Odeonspl. 18, 80539 Munich\n\nLunch in Munich Tambosi Photo Heatheronhertravels.com\n\nLunch in Munich at Tambosi\n\n## Best places to eat in Munich for dinner [...] > More info Hofbräuhaus website | Platzl 9, 80331 Munich\n\nWhere to eat in Munich - Hoffbrau beer Hall in Munich Photo Heatheronhertravels.com\n\nWhere to eat in Munich – Hofbrauhaus in Munich\n\n## Haxnbauer : Hearty German fare\n\nClose to the Hofbräuhaus is Haxnbauer, another well known spot for hearty Bavarian food. This is a good choice if you want a restaurant rather than beer hall. I’ve eaten there on a previous visit to Munich and it’s known for the huge pork knuckles that you can see roasting on the spit in the window.\n\nThe restaurant is large, busy and informal with a fun atmosphere, although more for meat eaters than vegetarians – read about my previous visit here\n\n> More info: Haxnbauer Website | Sparkassenstraße 6, 80331 Munich [...] Bar Buffet Kull in Munich\n\nWe also ordered a delicious tuna tartare as a starter which is their signature dishes and I also enjoyed my Aperol sour aperitif. This popular local alternative to the Aperol spritz has migrated everywhere from northern Italy.\n\nBar Buffet Kull is popular neighbourhood style restaurant that seemed to attract an elegant local crowd, with upscale bistro food that’s consistently good and a bustling atmosphere.\n\nAs an alternative for a nice dinner on Saturday night, you might like to try Brenner Grill (Maximilianstraße 15). Also part of the same restaurant family, it is close to the Residenz.",
      "score": 0.2435709,
      "raw_content": "[Skip to Content](#content)\n\n# Delicious Food in Munich Germany – where and what to eat\n\nYou may think the food in Munich is all about beer and pretzels. After all this is the capital of Bavaria, where the dishes are hearty and the beer flows freely. But if you are wondering what to eat in Munich, be aware there’s also a more sophisticated and cosmopolitan side to the Munich food scene, with plenty for foodies to enjoy.\n\nWe visited Munich on a short break so read on to discover where to eat in Munich for the most delicious German food.\n\n[![What to eat in Munich - Beer and pretzels](data:image/svg+xml,%3Csvg%20xmlns='http://www.w3.org/2000/svg'%20viewBox='0%200%201024%20768'%3E%3Crect%20width='1024'%20height='768'%20style='fill:%23e3e3e3'/%3E%3C/svg%3E)](https://www.heatheronhertravels.com/wp-content/uploads/2018/07/Beer-and-pretzels-in-Munich.jpg)\n\nWhat to eat in Munich – Beer and pretzels\n\nSummer in Munich means meeting up with friends in the biergarten for a Weissbier under the shady chestnut trees. Perhaps you’ll wander through the Viktualienmarkt admiring the fresh produce, stocking up for a picnic in the Hofgarten or stopping to buy a bratwurst from a street food stall?\n\n[![Where to eat in Munich - Lunch on the outdoor terraces - Read about delicious food in Munich ](https://www.heatheronhertravels.com/wp-content/uploads/2018/07/Munich-food-700.jpg.webp)](https://www.heatheronhertravels.com/wp-content/uploads/2018/07/Munich-food-700.jpg)\n\nWhere to eat in Munich – Lunch on the outdoor terraces\n\n*This article may contain affiliate links that provide commission on purchases you make at no extra cost to you. As an Amazon Associate I earn from qualifying purchases.*\n\nTable of Contents\n\n[Toggle](#)\n\n## Food options at Munich Airport\n\nWe arrived from Bristol and found Munich airport is a surprisingly good place to eat in Munich, with a choice of 60 different restaurants and cafés. Many offer food concepts that are unique to the airport. It’s a popular place for locals living nearby to come for dinner or for a bite to eat before their flight.\n\nIn the arrivals area is the Bayern Lounge where you can use the touch screens for information on things to do in Bavaria. Before you fly home pick up some regional specialties on sale in the adjoining café.\n\n> You may also enjoy: [15 of the best day trips from Munich by train](https://www.heatheronhertravels.com/best-day-trips-from-munich-germany/)\n\n[![Munich Airport Viewing Platform with BMI Regional Photo Heatheronhertravels.com](https://www.heatheronhertravels.com/wp-content/uploads/2018/07/Munich-Airport-Viewing-Platform-with-BMI-Regional-Photo-Heatheronhertravels.com_.jpg.webp)](https://www.heatheronhertravels.com/wp-content/uploads/2018/07/Munich-Airport-Viewing-Platform-with-BMI-Regional-Photo-Heatheronhertravels.com_.jpg)\n\nFood in Munich Airport – Cloud 7 cafe and the Munich Airport Viewing Platform\n\nThere’s a food court with lots of different restaurants and things to eat in Munich Airport on the first floor mezzanine. Up one more level is the Cloud 7 café, which has a cute retro theme and is decked out like the interior of an old plane.\n\nThe emphasis is on healthy food, with some treats of artisan ice cream and crêpes for desert. The café is on the observation deck where you can watch the planes come and go. Combine good coffee with plane-spotting and lots of space for the kids to run around.\n\n> Looking for a [**hotel near Munich Airport? Check out the best prices here**](https://www.heatheronhertravels.com/recommends/germany-hotels-munich-airport/)\n\n[![Where to eat in Munich Airport - Cloud 7 cafe with BMI Regional Photo Heatheronhertravels.com](https://www.heatheronhertravels.com/wp-content/uploads/2018/07/Munich-Airport-coffee-shop-with-BMI-Regional-Photo-Heatheronhertravels.com_.jpg.webp)](https://www.heatheronhertravels.com/wp-content/uploads/2018/07/Munich-Airport-coffee-shop-with-BMI-Regional-Photo-Heatheronhertravels.com_.jpg)\n\nWhere to eat in Munich Airport – Cloud 7 cafe\n\n## Ski-themed SportsAlm restaurant at Munich Airport\n\nWe also liked the SportsAlm restaurant at Munich Airport, which is on two levels and picks up the ski restaurant theme with authentic stonework and a cable car with a cosy table for two inside. Sit outside (albeit under the glass roof) and sun yourself on a deckchair, just as if you were taking a break on the ski slopes.\n\n[![Where to eat in Munich airport - Sportsalm restaurant with BMI Regional Photo Heatheronhertravels.com](https://www.heatheronhertravels.com/wp-content/uploads/2018/07/Munich-Airport-Restaurant-with-BMI-Regional-Photo-Heatheronhertravels.com_.jpg.webp)](https://www.heatheronhertravels.com/wp-content/uploads/2018/07/Munich-Airport-Restaurant-with-BMI-Regional-Photo-Heatheronhertravels.com_.jpg)\n\nWhere to eat in Munich airport – Sportsalm restaurant\n\nSadly there are no mountain views and you’ll be overlooking the main concourse. Here you can grab one of the favourite things to eat in Munich, a bratwurst from the silver retro Smoky Joe’s van that looks like a plane. It’s right next door the Europe’s only airport brewery, the [Airbrau](https://www.munich-airport.com/airbraeu-en-4431487) brewery, biergarten and restaurant where we stopped for a meal on the way back through the airport.\n\n> You may also enjoy: [20 fabulous things to do in Munich, Germany](https://www.heatheronhertravels.com/things-to-do-in-munich-germany/)\n\n[![Where to eat in Munich Airport - Bratwurst at Smoky Joes in Munich Airport with BMI Regional Photo Heatheronhertravels.com](https://www.heatheronhertravels.com/wp-content/uploads/2018/07/Munich-Airport-Smoky-Joes-with-BMI-Regional-Photo-Heatheronhertravels.com_.jpg.webp)](https://www.heatheronhertravels.com/wp-content/uploads/2018/07/Munich-Airport-Smoky-Joes-with-BMI-Regional-Photo-Heatheronhertravels.com_.jpg)\n\nWhere to eat in Munich Airport – Bratwurst at Smoky Joes in Munich Airport\n\n## The Viktualienmarkt in Munich\n\nIf you love to soak up the atmosphere of the local food markets on your travels, then the Viktualienmarkt in the centre of Munich is the place to wander on weekdays or on a Saturday morning (it’s closed on Sunday). This [**2 hour food tour of the Viktualienmarkt**](https://www.heatheronhertravels.com/recommends/germany-tourgyg-munich-gourmet-food-tour/) is a good way to taste the best of the market with an expert guide. Be sure to ask your guide for recommendations, as they will probably know the best restaurants in Munich too!\n\nMunich’s largest food market was originally situated in nearby Marienplatz but was moved to this site in 1807 when it outgrew the square. The market’s centre is marked by a maypole that has decorative figures to tell visitors what trades were available nearby.\n\n> **Read more:** [Munich outdoors – where to go cycling, surfing and wild swimming](https://www.heatheronhertravels.com/munich-outdoor-activities/ \"https://www.heatheronhertravels.com/athens4-hotel-athens-greece/\")\n\n[![Food in Munich at Viktualienmarkt food market Photo Heatheronhertravels.com](https://www.heatheronhertravels.com/wp-content/uploads/2018/07/Viktualienmarkt-in-Munich-Photo-Heatheronhertravels.com_.jpg.webp)](https://www.heatheronhertravels.com/wp-content/uploads/2018/07/Viktualienmarkt-in-Munich-Photo-Heatheronhertravels.com_.jpg)\n\nFood in Munich at Viktualienmarkt food market\n\nI love to look around food markets as a way to discover more about the local food culture. It’s the best way to find out what produce is in season, so that you can look out for it later on restaurant menus.\n\n[![Food in Munich - Viktualienmarkt in Munich with BMI Regional Photo Heatheronhertravels.com](https://www.heatheronhertravels.com/wp-content/uploads/2018/07/Viktualienmarkt-in-Munich-with-BMI-Regional-Photo-Heatheronhertravels.com_.jpg.webp)](https://www.heatheronhertravels.com/wp-content/uploads/2018/07/Viktualienmarkt-in-Munich-with-BMI-Regional-Photo-Heatheronhertravels.com_.jpg)\n\nFood in Munich – Viktualienmarkt in Munich\n\n## Fresh local produce at Viktualienmarkt\n\nWhen we visited the Viktualienmarkt it seemed to be all about the fat white stems of asparagus that were in season as well as artichokes and rosy spring radishes. If you visit at another time there may be different local produce in season, such as berries in summer or mushrooms in autumn.\n\n> You may also enjoy: [A food lover’s guide to Heidelberg in Germany](https://www.heatheronhertravels.com/german-food-in-heidelberg/)\n\n[![What to eat in Munich - Asparagus in the Viktualienmarkt Munich with BMI Regional Photo Heatheronhertravels.com](https://www.heatheronhertravels.com/wp-content/uploads/2018/07/Asparagus-in-the-Viktualienmarkt-Munich-with-BMI-Regional-Photo-Heatheronhertravels.com_.jpg.webp)](https://www.heatheronhertravels.com/wp-content/uploads/2018/07/Asparagus-in-the-Viktualienmarkt-Munich-with-BMI-Regional-Photo-Heatheronhertravels.com_.jpg)\n\nWhat to eat in Munich – Asparagus in the Viktualienmarkt Munich\n\nThe neighbourhood of a market is always a good place to find excellent restaurants since they have access to the freshest and best quality produce. There’s also a great biergarten at the Viktualienmarkt where you can sit under the chestnut trees. They don’t mind you consuming food that you might have bought at the market stalls, so long as you order a drink.\n\n> More info [Viktualienmarkt on Munich Tourism website](http://www.muenchen.de/int/en/shopping/markets/viktualienmarkt.html)\n\n[![Viktualienmarkt in Munich 2 Photo Heatheronhertravels.com](https://www.heatheronhertravels.com/wp-content/uploads/2018/07/Viktualienmarkt-in-Munich-2-Photo-Heatheronhertravels.com_.jpg.webp)](https://www.heatheronhertravels.com/wp-content/uploads/2018/07/Viktualienmarkt-in-Munich-2-Photo-Heatheronhertravels.com_.jpg)\n\nViktualienmarkt in Munich\n\n## Stop for a bratwurst in Munich\n\nThe Bavarian sausage or *wurst*, is a must-eat street food in Munich, served hot dog style in a fresh white roll with a squirt of mustard. Of course you’ll see *Bratwurst* (a sausage made with finely minced pork or veal) served throughout Germany, typically grilled over charcoal and served with *sauerkraut* (pickled cabbage) and mustard.\n\n> This [**2 hour food tour of the Viktualienmarkt**](https://www.heatheronhertravels.com/recommends/germany-tourgyg-munich-gourmet-food-tour/) is a good way to taste the best of the market with an expert guide.\n\nIn Munich the local variation is *Weisswurst* (white sausage), made with finely minced veal and spices. In the days before refrigeration it was made fresh each day without preservatives, hence it is traditionally eaten as a morning snack.\n\nThere’s a row of small shops selling sausages and meat products along one side of the Viktualmarkt. It’s a great place to get acquainted with all the local sausages and meat products in Munich.\n\n> Looking for a [**hotel in Munich? Check prices here**](https://www.heatheronhertravels.com/recommends/germany-hotels-munich/)\n\n[![What to eat in Munich - Bratwurst in the Viktualienmarkt in Munich with BMI Regional Photo Heatheronhertravels.com](https://www.heatheronhertravels.com/wp-content/uploads/2018/07/Bratwurst-in-the-Viktualienmarkt-in-Munich-with-BMI-Regional-Photo-Heatheronhertravels.com_.jpg.webp)](https://www.heatheronhertravels.com/wp-content/uploads/2018/07/Bratwurst-in-the-Viktualienmarkt-in-Munich-with-BMI-Regional-Photo-Heatheronhertravels.com_.jpg)\n\nWhat to eat in Munich – Bratwurst in the Viktualienmarkt in Munich\n\n## Beer and Pretzels in Munich\n\nWhile we’re on the topic of traditional German food, let’s talk about the ubiquitous beer and pretzels that you’ll find in every beer hall or biergarten. Beer is the drink of choice in Munich and my favourite is the Weissbier, the light coloured beer made from wheat with a slightly sweet flavour.\n\nWhile in Munich, we also enjoyed the Dunkel bier, a darker and more bitter style of beer. To line your stomach, where we might buy a packet of crisps in an English pub, the most popular snack in Munich is the pretzel which is sold in every beer hall.\n\n> Read more about [How to spend a weekend in Munich – in the sun or the rain](https://www.ontheluce.com/weekend-in-munich/) from On the Luce\n\n[![What to eat in Munich - Pretzel and Beer in Munich Photo Heatheronhertravels.com](https://www.heatheronhertravels.com/wp-content/uploads/2018/07/Pretzel-and-Beer-in-Munich-Photo-Heatheronhertravels.com_.jpg.webp)](https://www.heatheronhertravels.com/wp-content/uploads/2018/07/Pretzel-and-Beer-in-Munich-Photo-Heatheronhertravels.com_.jpg)\n\nWhat to eat in Munich – Pretzel and Beer in Munich\n\nIt’s a chewy ring of soft bread that’s liberally sprinkled with salt, designed to make you thirsty for more beer and a typical food to eat in Munich! The system in the biergarten and beer halls in Germany is to find a seat. Then the waiter or waitress (normally dressed in a traditional dirndl or lederhosen) will come to your table to take your order.\n\nWhen you are served you pay straight away in cash (often cards also accepted) and they will give you the change from the leather purse on their belt.\n\nIf you want to find the best beerhalls in Munich, try this [**evening of Bavarian beer and food culture**](https://www.heatheronhertravels.com/recommends/germany-tourgyg-munich-bavarian-beer-food-tour/) to discover the best traditional dishes and beer.\n\n## Hofbräuhaus Munich: The traditional beer hall of Munich\n\nIf you are wondering where to eat traditional German food in Munich, the popular choice which has become something of a tourist attraction is the Hofbräuhaus. It’s a huge beer hall with many different rooms as well as a large biergarten. The atmosphere is very jolly as you drink beer from a foaming stein (beer mug) and listen to the oomph band.\n\nThe food here is traditional Bavarian fare, like roast pork or beef soup with dumplings. Although the place is huge, the service seemed swift and efficient. Despite being the best known place for tourists to drink beer in Munich, there are also groups of regulars dressed in their lederhosen and it’s a fun atmosphere with locals and visitors mingling together.\n\n> More info [Hofbräuhaus website](https://www.hofbraeuhaus.de/en/) | Platzl 9, 80331 Munich\n\n[![Where to eat in Munich - Hoffbrau beer Hall in Munich Photo Heatheronhertravels.com](https://www.heatheronhertravels.com/wp-content/uploads/2018/07/Hoffbrau-beer-Hall-in-Munich-Photo-Heatheronhertravels.com_.jpg.webp)](https://www.heatheronhertravels.com/wp-content/uploads/2018/07/Hoffbrau-beer-Hall-in-Munich-Photo-Heatheronhertravels.com_.jpg)\n\nWhere to eat in Munich – Hofbrauhaus in Munich\n\n## Haxnbauer : Hearty German fare\n\nClose to the Hofbräuhaus is Haxnbauer, another well known spot for hearty Bavarian food. This is a good choice if you want a restaurant rather than beer hall. I’ve eaten there on a previous visit to Munich and it’s known for the huge pork knuckles that you can see roasting on the spit in the window.\n\nThe restaurant is large, busy and informal with a fun atmosphere, although more for meat eaters than vegetarians – [read about my previous visit here](https://www.heatheronhertravels.com/haxnbauer-munich-bavarian-specialities/)\n\n> More info: [Haxnbauer Website](https://www.kuffler.de/en/restaurant/haxnbauer/) | Sparkassenstraße 6, 80331 Munich\n\n[![What to eat in Munich - Pork knuckle at Haxnbauer in Munich Photo Heatheronhertravels.com](https://www.heatheronhertravels.com/wp-content/uploads/2018/07/Pok-knuckle-in-Munich-Photo-Heatheronhertravels.com_.jpg.webp)](https://www.heatheronhertravels.com/wp-content/uploads/2018/07/Pok-knuckle-in-Munich-Photo-Heatheronhertravels.com_.jpg)\n\nWhat to eat in Munich – Pork knuckle at Haxnbauer in Munich\n\n## Where to find the best biergarten in Munich\n\nAnother German institution which you’ll especially enjoy in Munich is the *biergarten* or beer garden. It’s an open air terrace where best traditional food and beer in Munich is served.\n\nSince the summers in Munich can be quite warm, the biergarten are often situated under shady trees, or in parks. It’s a delightful way to enjoy the outdoor lifestyle of Southern Germany.\n\n> If you want to get an easy overview of all the sights in Munich, check out this [**Hop on Hop off bus tour**](https://www.heatheronhertravels.com/recommends/munich-tourgyg-hop-on-hop-off-bus-tour/)\n\n## Chineseturn biergarten in Munich\n\nI’ve already mentioned the biergarten at the Viktualienmarkt which is very close to Marienplatz. Our favourite biergarten from the trip was at the [Chinesischer Turm](https://www.chinaturm.de/) or Chinese Pagoda in the Englischer Garten Munich. The pagoda is a landmark in this huge park in the centre of Munich.\n\nThe bus stops here, although you’ll enjoy the park more if you arrive on foot or bicycle. You could also hire one of the cycle rickshaws or horse and carriage rides that run through the park.\n\n[![Chinese biegarden in Munich with Fly BMI Photo Heatheronhertravels.com](https://www.heatheronhertravels.com/wp-content/uploads/2018/07/Chinese-biegarden-in-Munich-with-Fly-BMI-Photo-Heatheronhertravels.com_.jpg.webp)](https://www.heatheronhertravels.com/wp-content/uploads/2018/07/Chinese-biegarden-in-Munich-with-Fly-BMI-Photo-Heatheronhertravels.com_.jpg)\n\nChinese biergarten in Munich\n\nThe Chinese Pagoda sits at the centre of the biergarten, with tables and benches set around it under the shady chestnut trees. It’s an ideal spot for lunch in the summer months and even though it’s hugely popular there always seems to be a place to sit.\n\nYou can buy your lunch at the self-service café areas where they serve Bavarian traditional dishes. We tried a selection of sausage and sauerkraut, potato salad, white sausage.\n\nAlso cheese pté seasoned with paprika called *Obatzda* and of course lots and lots of beer. If you want something a bit more refined there’s also an elegant restaurant with indoor seating and table service.\n\n[![German dishes in the Chinese biergarten in Munich Photo Heatheronhertravels.com](https://www.heatheronhertravels.com/wp-content/uploads/2018/07/German-dishes-in-the-Chinese-biergarten-in-Munich-Photo-Heatheronhertravels.com_.jpg.webp)](https://www.heatheronhertravels.com/wp-content/uploads/2018/07/German-dishes-in-the-Chinese-biergarten-in-Munich-Photo-Heatheronhertravels.com_.jpg)\n\nGerman dishes in the Chinese biergarten in Munich\n\nAfter lunch we cycled on through the Englischer Garten towards the Seehaus Biergarten beside the Kleinhesseloher lake. This is another popular biergarten with a similar self service café, where you can sit under the trees with a view of the lake. Nearby you can hire a rowing boat or pedalo to have some fun on the lake.\n\n> If you’d like to explore Munich by bike, check out this [**3 hour guided bike tour to see the city**](https://www.heatheronhertravels.com/recommends/germany-tourgyg-munich-3-hour-bike-tour/)\n\n[![Biergarten in the Englischergarten Munich Photo Heatheronhertravels.com](https://www.heatheronhertravels.com/wp-content/uploads/2018/07/Biergarten-in-the-Englischergarten-Munich-Photo-Heatheronhertravels.com_.jpg.webp)](https://www.heatheronhertravels.com/wp-content/uploads/2018/07/Biergarten-in-the-Englischergarten-Munich-Photo-Heatheronhertravels.com_.jpg)\n\nSeehaus Biergarten in the Englischergarten Munich\n\n## Best Munich restaurants for lunch\n\nYou may be forgiven for thinking from what I’ve described so far, that all you’ll find in Munich is hearty Bavarian fare. In fact the city has a sophisticated and cosmopolitan restaurant scene. If you’re looking for an elegant but informal place to have lunch, here are a couple of options that we enjoyed;\n\n> You may also enjoy: [3 days in Munich – our itinerary of cool things to see and do](https://www.heatheronhertravels.com/3-days-in-munich-our-itinerary-of-things-to-do/)\n\n## Bar Centrale in Munich\n\nThis popular Italian bar is right opposite [**Hotel Cortiina**](https://www.heatheronhertravels.com/recommends/munich-hotels-cortiina/) where we stayed. It’s part of the same family of hotels, bars and restaurants that serve some of the best food in Munich. A stone’s throw from the Hofbräuhaus but you’d probably not notice it, unless by the crowds of locals that spill out onto the pavement for an after-work drink.\n\nThere’s an understated yet stylish atmosphere here, like an Italian backstreet bar that’s somehow been transplanted to Germany. Bar Centrale is open through the day with a small menu of freshly cooked pasta dishes coming from the tiny kitchen.\n\nHopefully you can find a place to sit in the back room. We managed to get a seat and enjoyed the seafood linguine in a wine sauce, chosen from the blackboard menu that they bring around to you.\n\n> More Info: [Bar Centrale Website](http://www.bar-centrale.com) | Ledererstraße 23 80331 Munich\n\n[![Pasta in Bar Centrale in Munich Photo Heatheronhertravels.com](https://www.heatheronhertravels.com/wp-content/uploads/2018/07/Pasta-in-Bar-Centrale-in-Munich-Photo-Heatheronhertravels.com_.jpg.webp)](https://www.heatheronhertravels.com/wp-content/uploads/2018/07/Pasta-in-Bar-Centrale-in-Munich-Photo-Heatheronhertravels.com_.jpg)\n\nPasta in Bar Centrale in Munich\n\n## Tambosi Restaurant at the Hofgarten\n\nLooking for a place to lunch with a biergarten atmosphere but fancy something a bit more sophisticated than bratwurst and sauerkraut? Head to the Hofgarten and the Tambosi outdoor terrace.\n\nThe Hofgarten is a beautiful formal garden that was once a private space of the Wittelsbach rulers and their court. Now it’s a public park, with fountains, parterres and a central pavilion.\n\n> You may also enjoy: [Coberg – Victoria and Albert’s romantic retreat in Germany](https://www.heatheronhertravels.com/coburg-victoria-and-albert-in-germany/)\n\nWe enjoyed our al fresco lunch here on the Tambosi restaurant terrace, watching the groups playing boules and the cyclists passing by. The menu was Italian inspired with pizza and pasta, as well as colourful salads served with fresh bread and beer. Prices reflected the prime location, but it was a very pretty and stylish place for lunch on a fine summer’s day.\n\n> More info: [Tambosi Website](http://tambosi-odeonsplatz.de) | Odeonspl. 18, 80539 Munich\n\n[![Lunch in Munich Tambosi Photo Heatheronhertravels.com](https://www.heatheronhertravels.com/wp-content/uploads/2018/07/Lunch-in-Munich-Tambosi-Photo-Heatheronhertravels.com_.jpg.webp)](https://www.heatheronhertravels.com/wp-content/uploads/2018/07/Lunch-in-Munich-Tambosi-Photo-Heatheronhertravels.com_.jpg)\n\nLunch in Munich at Tambosi\n\n## Best places to eat in Munich for dinner\n\nIf you are looking for the best restaurants in Munich for dinner, we can recommend the informal but excellent Bar Buffet Kull. It’s just around the corner from our Hotel Cortiina and close to the Viktualienmarkt. Remember what I said about great restaurants being found near the market?\n\n[Bar Buffet Kull](https://www.buffet-kull.de/ \"Bar Buffet Kull\") is Munich’s answer to the classic French bistro, with red checked table cloths and tables packed closely together. The dishes are simply prepared and presented. I tried a delicious pan-fried wild salmon with the sweet white asparagus that was in season with melted butter.\n\n> You may also enjoy: [Top 10 things to do in Heidelberg](https://www.heatheronhertravels.com/things-to-do-in-heidelberg-video/)\n\n[![Dinner in Munich Photo Heatheronhertravels.com](https://www.heatheronhertravels.com/wp-content/uploads/2018/07/Dinner-in-Munich-Photo-Heatheronhertravels.com_.jpg.webp)](https://www.heatheronhertravels.com/wp-content/uploads/2018/07/Dinner-in-Munich-Photo-Heatheronhertravels.com_.jpg)\n\nBar Buffet Kull in Munich\n\nWe also ordered a delicious tuna tartare as a starter which is their signature dishes and I also enjoyed my Aperol sour aperitif. This popular local alternative to the Aperol spritz has migrated everywhere from northern Italy.\n\nBar Buffet Kull is popular neighbourhood style restaurant that seemed to attract an elegant local crowd, with upscale bistro food that’s consistently good and a bustling atmosphere.\n\nAs an alternative for a nice dinner on Saturday night, you might like to try [Brenner Grill](https://www.brennergrill.de/) (Maximilianstraße 15). Also part of the same restaurant family, it is close to the Residenz.\n\nIt’s a large bar and brasserie with a stylish atmosphere and outdoor terrace. Much of the fish and meat is prepared over a charcoal grill but there are good veggie options too.\n\n> Looking for a [**hotel in Munich? Check prices here**](https://www.heatheronhertravels.com/recommends/germany-hotels-munich/)\n\n[![Dinner in Munich Photo Heatheronhertravels.com](https://www.heatheronhertravels.com/wp-content/uploads/2018/07/Dinner-in-Munich-2-Photo-Heatheronhertravels.com_.jpg.webp)](https://www.heatheronhertravels.com/wp-content/uploads/2018/07/Dinner-in-Munich-2-Photo-Heatheronhertravels.com_.jpg)\n\nBar Buffet Kull in Munich\n\n## Wine Bars in Munich – try Grapes Weinbar\n\nFor food that’s light and elegant, with a well chosen glass of wine, you’ll enjoy [Grapes Weinbar](https://www.grapes-weinbar.de/en/ \"Grapes Weinbar\") which was next to our Hotel Cortiina. This is where hotel guests are served breakfast. Later it turns into an all day café and in the evening into an elegant wine bar.\n\nThere are small plates for nibbles and bistro style dishes such as smoked trout fillet, beef tartare, terrines and sorbets. If the weather is fine there’s a small terrace at the back and they open the windows wide onto the street.\n\n[![Grapes Weinbar in Munich Photo Heatheronhertravels.com](https://www.heatheronhertravels.com/wp-content/uploads/2018/05/Grapes-Weinbar-in-Munich-Photo-Heatheronhertravels.com_.jpg.webp)](https://www.heatheronhertravels.com/wp-content/uploads/2018/05/Grapes-Weinbar-in-Munich-Photo-Heatheronhertravels.com_.jpg)\n\nGrapes Weinbar in Munich\n\n## For drinks and cocktails in Munich – Die Goldene Bar\n\nAnother great option if you are looking for somewhere atmospheric for drinks is [Die Goldene Bar](http://www.goldenebar.de/). It is located at the back of Haus der Kunst and has a terrace overlooking the Englischer Garten.\n\nThe bar is known for the golden wall maps from 1937 which give the bar its name. They show the places that the spirits and tobacco came from around the world.\n\n[![Die Goldene Bar in Munich with FlyBMI Photo Heatheronhertravels.com](https://www.heatheronhertravels.com/wp-content/uploads/2018/07/Die-Goldene-Bar-in-Munich-with-FlyBMI-Photo-Heatheronhertravels.com_.jpg.webp)](https://www.heatheronhertravels.com/wp-content/uploads/2018/07/Die-Goldene-Bar-in-Munich-with-FlyBMI-Photo-Heatheronhertravels.com_.jpg)\n\nDie Goldene Bar in Munich\n\nWe visited in the afternoon and had a drink on the terrace. I thought the original bar would be a fabulous place for an evening cocktail or aperitif. They also have a menu of small plates, snacks and sandwiches.\n\n[![Drinks at The Golden Bar in Munich Photo Heatheronhertravels.com](https://www.heatheronhertravels.com/wp-content/uploads/2018/07/Drinks-at-The-Golden-Bar-in-Munich-Photo-Heatheronhertravels.com_.jpg.webp)](https://www.heatheronhertravels.com/wp-content/uploads/2018/07/Drinks-at-The-Golden-Bar-in-Munich-Photo-Heatheronhertravels.com_.jpg)\n\nDrinks at The Golden Bar in Munich\n\n## Airbrau – the brewery at Munich Airport\n\nWe may have been heading home after our weekend in Munich but there was still time for a last taste of Munich at the airport before our flight. We stopped by at [Airbrau](https://www.munich-airport.com/airbraeu-en-4431487) which has a restaurant and biergarten in the outdoor concourse of the airport under the lofty glazed roof.\n\n[![Airbrau in Munich Photo Heatheronhertravels.com](https://www.heatheronhertravels.com/wp-content/uploads/2018/07/Airbrau-in-Munich-Photo-Heatheronhertravels.com_.jpg.webp)](https://www.heatheronhertravels.com/wp-content/uploads/2018/07/Airbrau-in-Munich-Photo-Heatheronhertravels.com_.jpg)\n\nAirbrau in Munich\n\nIt’s the only brewery that’s located inside an airport and you can see the shiny copper vats inside the restaurant. They serve a selection of different beers that are brewed on the premises.\n\n[![Airbrau in Munich airport with FlyBMI Photo Heatheronhrtravels.com](https://www.heatheronhertravels.com/wp-content/uploads/2018/07/Airbrau-in-Munich-airport-2-with-FlyBMI-Photo-Heatheronhrtravels.com_.jpg.webp)](https://www.heatheronhertravels.com/wp-content/uploads/2018/07/Airbrau-in-Munich-airport-2-with-FlyBMI-Photo-Heatheronhrtravels.com_.jpg)\n\nAirbrau in Munich airport\n\nThe menu has lots of Bavarian specialties and I enjoyed the trout, with parsley potatoes and the sweet white asparagus that was in season. You can even do a brewery tour here with the brewery master. This takes place most days except Sunday and it’s the place to get your last taste of Bavarian beer before you fly home.\n\n[![Airbrau in Munich airport with FlyBMI Photo Heatheronhrtravels.com](https://www.heatheronhertravels.com/wp-content/uploads/2018/07/Airbrau-in-Munich-airport-with-FlyBMI-Photo-Heatheronhrtravels.com_.jpg.webp)](https://www.heatheronhertravels.com/wp-content/uploads/2018/07/Airbrau-in-Munich-airport-with-FlyBMI-Photo-Heatheronhrtravels.com_.jpg)\n\nAirbrau in Munich airport\n\n## Hotels in Munich – Hotel Cortiina\n\nWhen visiting a city like Munich for a short break I love to stay in a centrally located boutique hotel like [**Hotel Cortiina**](https://www.heatheronhertravels.com/recommends/munich-hotels-cortiina/ \"Hotel Cortiina\"). We flew in from Bristol on an early morning flight and were able take the train into the centre. Leaving our luggage at the hotel, we were out enjoying the city within the space of an hour.\n\n[**Hotel Cortiina**](https://www.heatheronhertravels.com/recommends/munich-hotels-cortiina/) is located very centrally just 5 minutes walk from Marienplatz, on a charming side street. There’s quirky independent shops like the gift shop opposite selling dachshund gifts and another devoted to Cuban cigars. The hotel is inspired by Italian style blended with subtle Asian elements.\n\nIt’s actually a Feng Shui hotel and it made sense that one of the owners is an architect and designer. Clean lines combine with the use of rich patterns and textures, creating a warm and elegant look.\n\n[![Cortiina Hotel Munich Photo Heatheronhertravels.com](https://www.heatheronhertravels.com/wp-content/uploads/2018/05/Cortiina-Hotel-Munich-Photo-Heatheronhertravels.com_.jpg.webp)](https://www.heatheronhertravels.com/wp-content/uploads/2018/05/Cortiina-Hotel-Munich-Photo-Heatheronhertravels.com_.jpg)\n\nCortiina Hotel Munich\n\nOur bathroom was wall to ceiling marble, with a powerful walk in shower. The waxed paper door screen echoed a Japanese style. The 4th floor room where we stayed was in an apartment annex next to the main hotel entrance. It had its own doorway from the street.\n\nStay at the [Cortiina Hotel](https://www.heatheronhertravels.com/recommends/munich-hotels-cortiina/) in Munich\n\n[Check prices and availability](https://www.heatheronhertravels.com/recommends/munich-hotels-cortiina/)\n\nSearch for other [hotels in Munich](https://www.heatheronhertravels.com/recommends/germany-hotels-munich/)\n\n## Breakfast at Grapes Weinbar\n\nWe had breakfast in the Grapes Weinbar next to reception, which served an exceptional breakfast, beautifully presented on a long table. There was a wide range of delicious things to try, from the hot dish of Turkish eggs, to the local artisan cheeses. Also freshly squeezed orange juice, outstanding coffee and array of nuts and seeds to sprinkle on your fruit or muesli.\n\n[![Hotel Cortiina Munich Photo Heatheronhertravels.com](https://www.heatheronhertravels.com/wp-content/uploads/2018/05/Hotel-Cortiina-Munich-Photo-Heatheronhertravels.com_.jpg.webp)](https://www.heatheronhertravels.com/wp-content/uploads/2018/05/Hotel-Cortiina-Munich-Photo-Heatheronhertravels.com_.jpg)\n\nHotel Cortiina Munich\n\nThe small internal courtyard was perfect to sit on sunny days leading through to a well equipped gym. Once breakfast was over, the Grapes Weinbar transformed into an all day café and evening wine bar. They served light dishes, wine and other drinks.\n\nWe loved the impeccable sense of style and very professional and friendly service at Hotel Cortiina. It is part of a small group of hotels and restaurants, such as Bar Centrale across the road and Buffet Kass Bar nearby, both of which we tried.\n\n> More info [Hotel Cortiina Website](https://www.cortiina.com/en/) | [**Compare prices and book your stay**](https://www.heatheronhertravels.com/recommends/munich-hotels-cortiina/ \"Compare prices and book your stay\")\n\n[![Hotel Cortiina in Munich Photo Heatheronhertravels.com](https://www.heatheronhertravels.com/wp-content/uploads/2018/07/Hotel-Cortiina-in-Munich-Photo-Heatheronhertravels.com_.jpg.webp)](https://www.heatheronhertravels.com/wp-content/uploads/2018/07/Hotel-Cortiina-in-Munich-Photo-Heatheronhertravels.com_.jpg)\n\nHotel Cortiina in Munich\n\n> ## Read Next\n>\n> Plan your [3 days in Munich – our Munich itinerary of cool things to see and do](https://www.heatheronhertravels.com/3-days-in-munich-our-itinerary-of-things-to-do/)\n>\n> ## More Munich articles\n>\n> [20 fabulous things to do in Munich, Germany](https://www.heatheronhertravels.com/things-to-do-in-munich-germany/ \"20 fabulous things to do in Munich, Germany\")  \n> [15 of the best day trips from Munich by train](https://www.heatheronhertravels.com/best-day-trips-from-munich-germany/)  \n> [Visiting the Munich Christmas Markets – Podcast](https://www.heatheronhertravels.com/visiting-the-munich-christmas-markets-podcast-episode-4/)\n>\n> ## You may also enjoy\n>\n> [Taste the food of Alabama – 20 dishes you’ll want to try!](https://www.heatheronhertravels.com/alabama-food-usa/)  \n> [Delicious PEI food – what and where to eat in Prince Edward Island, Canada](https://www.heatheronhertravels.com/pei-food-in-canada/)\n\n## More information for planning your trip to Munich\n\nLooking for a [**hotel in Munich? Check prices here**](https://www.heatheronhertravels.com/recommends/germany-hotels-munich/).\n\nCheck out the [Munich Airport website](https://www.munich-airport.com/) for all the things to see and do at this 5 star airport.\n\nNeed a guidebook for Munich? We recommend the [DK Eyewitness Top 10 Munich](https://www.heatheronhertravels.com/recommends/munich-books-dk-top-10/) guide\n\n[Buy Now](https://www.heatheronhertravels.com/recommends/munich-books-dk-top-10/)\n\nCheck out the [Munich Tourism website](https://www.munich.travel/en) for more information to help plan your visit to Munich.\n\nCheck out the [Germany Tourism website](http://www.germany.travel/en/index.html) if you are planning to visit other parts of Germany\n\n## Getting from Munich airport to the city centre\n\nYou can take both bus and train from Munich Airport into the city centre. We decided to take the train as it was very easy and straightforward.\n\nFrom the airport concourse you go straight down into the train station and take the S8 train. It stops at Marienplatz and other train stations in the centre of the city.\n\nAn alternative is the S1 train which takes a different route and takes a little longer to Marienplatz. Buy your ticket from the ticket machine on the station platform with cash or card. The train goes every 20 minutes and the journey is around 40 minutes.\n\n> More info: [Munich Airport Website](https://www.munich-airport.com/public-transport-260822)\n\nAnother option is the [**Airport Bus**](https://www.heatheronhertravels.com/recommends/germany-tourgyg-munich-airport-bus-transfer/) which runs every 15 minutes and offers a non-stop service to the city centre. You can use the bus no matter what airline you fly with.\n\n## Pin It\n\nI received complimentary flights, hotel and activities from airlines, Munich Airport, Hotel Cortiina as part of a sponsored\\* visit to Munich.\n\n\\* More info on my [policies page](https://www.heatheronhertravels.com/work-with-me/policies/)\n\nThis article is originally published at [Heatheronhertravels.com](https://www.heatheronhertravels.com/)\n\n## [Never miss an update! – Subscribe to receive our latest articles and newsletter](https://www.heatheronhertravels.com/recommends/subscribe/ \"Never miss an update! - Subscribe to receive our latest articles and newsletter\")\n\nAndrew Lee\n\nSaturday 31st of August 2019\n\nOff to Munich for a quick business trip.       "raw_content": "[Skip to main content](https://www.getyourguide.com/#main-content-wrapper)\n\n[![Image 1: Get Your Guide logo](blob:https://www.getyourguide.com/996937e112db2dceea035d3e64f5bf6e)](https://www.getyourguide.com/ \"GetYourGuide\")\n*    \n*   EN/USD $ \n*   [Profile](https://www.getyourguide.com/# \"Profile\") \n\n*   [Explore Germany](https://www.getyourguide.com/germany-l169009/)\n\n*   Places to see \n*   Things to do \n*   Trip inspiration \n\n1.   [The Explorer](https://www.getyourguide.com/explorer/)\n2.   [Things to do in Germany](https://www.getyourguide.com/germany-l169009/ttd/)\n3.   [Germany Travel Guide](https://www.getyourguide.com/explorer/germany-ttd169009/)\n\n# The best food in Germany: what to try and where to go\n\nFrom indulgent cheesy noodles to the iconic currywurst, here are the ultimate German foods.\n\n[![Image 2: influencer-profile-image](https://cdn.getyourguide.com/image/format=auto%2Cfit=crop%2Cgravity=auto%2Cquality=60%2Cwidth=100%2Cheight=100/dam/Kiawna%20Brewster_crop.png)](https://www.getyourguide.com/explorer/creators/kiawna-brewster/)\n\nKiawna Brewster\n\nJun 23, 2026•13 min read\n\nShare on\n\n![Image 3: The best food in Germany: what to try and where to go](https://cdn.getyourguide.com/image/format=avif%2Cfit=cover%2Cgravity=auto%2Cquality=60/dam/BCC-2024-EXPLORER-GERMANY-FOOD-IN-GERMANY-HEADER_DESKTOP.jpg)\n\nFood in Germany goes far beyond beer and bratwurst. Across the country, regional dishes built around simple ingredients like meat, potatoes, and fresh bread appear in different forms wherever you go.   \nIn cities like [Frankfurt](https://www.getyourguide.com/frankfurt-l21/ttd/) or Berlin, you’ll find käsespätzle in local taverns, pretzels served with beer in traditional halls, and gingerbread at winter markets. This guide covers some of the most popular foods to try in [Germany](https://www.getyourguide.com/germany-l169009/) and where to find them.\n\n## 1. Bratwurst\n\n![Image 4: A German bratwurst in a bun topped with mustard, served alongside sauerkraut.](https://cdn.getyourguide.com/image/format=auto%2Cfit=cover%2Cgravity=auto%2Cquality=60/dam/kelsey-todd-GeeTx3vuMOM-unsplash-LOWRES.jpg)\nDiscover rich flavors with a classic German bratwurst.\n\nWhat food is Germany known for? It’s the iconic bratwurst, a sausage made from pork, beef, or veal that dates back to the 14th century — it was first mentioned in a document from Nuremberg in 1313. Typically seasoned with a blend of spices, including marjoram, caraway, nutmeg, coriander, and ginger, before being grilled or pan-fried, bratwurst is beloved for its juicy, flavorful meat.\n\n### How to eat the best bratwurst in Germany?\n\nYou can find exciting regional variations of bratwurst across Germany. Popular options include the _Nürnberger rostbratwurst_ from Nuremberg, which is a small, thin sausage that’s grilled over beechwood fire, and the _Thüringer rostbratwurst_ from [Thuringia](https://www.getyourguide.com/thuringia-l239/), a large and coarse version that’s grilled over charcoal.\n\n## Find local food tours in Nuremberg\n\n*   [![Image 5: image](https://cdn.getyourguide.com/image/format=auto%2Cfit=cover%2Cgravity=auto%2Cquality=60%2Cwidth=270%2Cheight=180%2Cdpr=2/tour_img/64afbce001c87.jpeg) Bamberg: Guided Historical Culinary Tour Learn about the city of Bamberg and its rich brewing and culinary heritage on a guided walking tour. Taste some local delicacies, wine, and beer on your journey through local history. 4.5 (622) From$28](https://www.getyourguide.com/bamberg-l2537/hornla-bier-und-zwiebeltreter-kulinarisches-aus-bamberg-t488145/?ranking_uuid=10a61c73-f3cb-47e3-bbac-c015ee05af05) \n*   [![Image 6: image](https://cdn.getyourguide.com/image/format=auto%2Cfit=cover%2Cgravity=auto%2Cquality=60%2Cwidth=270%2Cheight=180%2Cdpr=2/tour_img/a8700907b935a690888f9aa056562ec9b822ee0d165ff19cbfc38ec288a53ff0.jpg) Nuremberg: Castle and Old Town Tour – incl. tasting of original Nuremberg gingerbread Guided tour of Nuremberg's most beautiful and most important sites in the Old Town. Experience a journey back in time to the 15th century, when great artists, emperors, patrician families and philosophers shaped this city. 4.9 (93) From$77](https://www.getyourguide.com/nuremberg-l127/nuremberg-castle-and-old-town-tour-incl-entrance-tickets-and-red-beer-tasting-t982081/?ranking_uuid=10a61c73-f3cb-47e3-bbac-c015ee05af05) \n*   [![Image 7: image](https://cdn.getyourguide.com/image/format=auto%2Cfit=cover%2Cgravity=auto%2Cquality=60%2Cwidth=270%2Cheight=180%2Cdpr=2/tour_img/dcf8b85c2beeb02e.jpeg) Nuremberg: Food History Walk, \"Rotes Bier\" & \"Blaue Zipfel\"Learn about the city of Nuremberg and its rich culinary heritage on a guided walking tour. Taste some local delicacies on your journey through local history. 4.4 (236) From$28](https://www.getyourguide.com/nuremberg-l127/red-beer-and-blue-tips-a-culinary-walk-through-nuremberg-t516890/?ranking_uuid=10a61c73-f3cb-47e3-bbac-c015ee05af05) \n*    \n\n## 2. Brezel\n\n![Image 8: A freshly baked Bavarian pretzel on a traditional blue and white checkered napkin.](https://cdn.getyourguide.com/image/format=auto%2Cfit=cover%2Cgravity=auto%2Cquality=60/dam/bavaria-3501921-LOWRES.jpg)\nEnjoy the popular Bavarian pretzel, a German staple.\n\nYou’ve likely had a pretzel, the English word for brezel, but there’s nothing like having one in Germany with an ice-cold beer. This twisted baked bread has a distinctive glossy brown crust, achieved by dipping the dough in a lye (sodium hydroxide) solution before baking. The interior is soft and chewy, making it a delightful contrast to the crisp exterior. They can be salted or unsalted and come in various sizes, from small snacks to plate-sized versions.\n\n### How to eat the best brezel in Germany?\n\nTry the most iconic version, the _Bayerische brezel_ (Bavarian pretzel), known for its large size and thick, doughy arms. Head to the Hofbrӓuhaus Brewery, one of the famous Munich food spots, and order your brezel with a Mȕnchner Hell (a Munich lager) on the side.\n\n## Book a brewery tour in Munich\n\n*   [![Image 9: image](https://cdn.getyourguide.com/image/format=auto%2Cfit=cover%2Cgravity=auto%2Cquality=60%2Cwidth=270%2Cheight=180%2Cdpr=2/tour_img/e076ccf42e47628c39dd6f9d0aee5da15c4eb467a388a6985ae255c07a35f65b.jpg) Munich: Old Town Food Tour with 10+ Tastings, Beer & Pretzel Taste 10+ Bavarian specialties in Munich’s open-air market & historic beerhalls. From pretzels to weisswurst & beer, explore the city’s culinary heart with local stories & flavors. 4.7 (193) From$116](https://www.getyourguide.com/munich-l26/munich-old-town-food-tour-with-10-tastings-beer-pretzel-t923331/?ranking_uuid=ecac53c1-dfc8-4658-9248-95500912c9c0) \n*   [![Image 10: image](https://cdn.getyourguide.com/image/format=auto%2Cfit=cover%2Cgravity=auto%2Cquality=60%2Cwidth=270%2Cheight=180%2Cdpr=2/tour_img/5d9c8f1996f9c.jpeg) Munich: Beer and Food Tour with Dinner & Oktoberfest Museum Visit the heart of Munich's food culture and spend the evening sampling the best of traditional food and beer. Enjoy a guided tour with a visit to the Oktoberfest Museum and a Bavarian dinner. 4.7 (1,217) From$81](https://www.getyourguide.com/munich-l26/munich-an-evening-of-bavarian-beer-and-food-culture-t24191/?ranking_uuid=ecac53c1-dfc8-4658-9248-95500912c9c0) \n*   [![Image 11: image](https://cdn.getyourguide.com/image/format=auto%2Cfit=cover%2Cgravity=auto%2Cquality=60%2Cwidth=270%2Cheight=180%2Cdpr=2/tour_img/5225c6dea384b6548e4de56f5bc05366fa0b147c24cb9ae6f79a5513c24681a6.jpg) Munich: Bavarian Food Tour with Full Meal & Beer Tastings Embark on a Munich guided food tour and savor Weißwurst, Leberkässemmel, traditional Bavarian mains, sweet pastries, and local drinks, while discovering the city’s rich culinary traditions. 4.8 (57) From$122$86](https://www.getyourguide.com/munich-l26/munich-food-tour-true-bavarian-full-meal-with-drinks-t865768/?ranking_uuid=ecac53c1-dfc8-4658-9248-95500912c9c0) \n*    \n\n## 3. Kartoffelsalat\n\n![Image 12: German potato salad with sliced potatoes, onions, and herbs in a bowl.](https://cdn.getyourguide.com/image/format=auto%2Cfit=cover%2Cgravity=auto%2Cquality=60/dam/potato-salad-4836398-LOWRES.jpg)\nDive into tangy German potato salad, a classic side.\n\nWhat is Germany food known for? Well, Germans’ love for potatoes runs deep. If you had to choose just one potato-based food to eat, make it _kartoffelsalat._ This salad is a beloved side dish that has been part of German cuisine for centuries, dating back to the introduction of the potato to Europe in the late 16th century. It typically consists of boiled potatoes mixed with ingredients, such as onions, bacon, pickles, mustard, and fresh herbs, and dressed in a vinegar-based or mayonnaise dressing.\n\n### How to eat the best kartoffelsalat in Germany?\n\nTaste as many regional variations as you can. In southern Germany, such as [Bavaria](https://www.getyourguide.com/bavaria-l241/) and Swabia, it’s typically tangy and savory, made with a warm vinegar-based dressing. In the north, it’s usually creamier and richer with a mayonnaise-based dressing.\n\n## Explore local food tours in Bavaria\n\n*   [![Image 13: image](https://cdn.getyourguide.com/image/format=auto%2Cfit=cover%2Cgravity=auto%2Cquality=60%2Cwidth=270%2Cheight=180%2Cdpr=2/tour_img/76ea314ff89511c270c0d64a6a2ae02cc4a6fe455e48a5a2e3a75f569c5fbb58.png) Munich: Authentic street food with Peter – Viktualienmarkt & hidden spots Small group! Join me, Peter, to explore the Old Town and its surrounding neighborhoods on a street food & history tour starting at Marienplatz. I'll take you to local delicacies and my insider tips… 5 (13) From$44$39](https://www.getyourguide.com/munich-l26/peter-s-trendy-neighborhood-platzl-viktualienmarkt-street-food-cafe-tour-4h-t1170964/?ranking_uuid=af39c9d0-5815-4a8f-9803-0b7304c5dcb2) \n*   [![Image 14: image](https://cdn.getyourguide.com/image/format=auto%2Cfit=cover%2Cgravity=auto%2Cquality=60%2Cwidth=270%2Cheight=180%2Cdpr=2/tour_img/943ed434d6d8e33ba9f09b0e4e4009f9beea5fca31737ec3f807aefc868bf303.jpg) Munich: Viktualienmarkt Food Tour Enjoy a guided tour of the Viktualienmarkt in Munich and taste delicious regional foods as well as international delicacies. Enjoy the bustling life of the market and learn more about local cuisine. 4.5 (1,329) From$48](https://www.getyourguide.com/munich-l26/viktualienmarkt-2-hour-gourmet-food-tour-munich-t21376/?ranking_uuid=af39c9d0-5815-4a8f-9803-0b7304c5dcb2) \n*   [![Image 15: image](https://cdn.getyourguide.com/image/format=auto%2Cfit=cover%2Cgravity=auto%2Cquality=60%2Cwidth=270%2Cheight=180%2Cdpr=2/tour_img/5225c6dea384b6548e4de56f5bc05366fa0b147c24cb9ae6f79a5513c24681a6.jpg) Munich: Bavarian Food Tour with Full Meal & Beer Tastings Embark on a Munich guided food tour and savor Weißwurst, Leberkässemmel, traditional Bavarian mains, sweet pastries, and local drinks, while discovering the city’s rich culinary traditions. 4.8 (57) From$122$86](https://www.getyourguide.com/munich-l26/munich-food-tour-true-bavarian-full-meal-with-drinks-t865768/?ranking_uuid=af39c9d0-5815-4a8f-9803-0b7304c5dcb2) \n*    \n\n## 4. Kӓsespӓtzle\n\n![Image 16: A plate of käsespätzle, a cheesy noodle dish garnished with fried onions and chives.](https://cdn.getyourguide.com/image/format=auto%2Cfit=cover%2Cgravity=auto%2Cquality=60/dam/bavaria-5372559-LOWRES.jpg)\nIndulge in käsespätzle, a cheesy German delight.\n\nOften referred to as Germany’s version of macaroni and cheese, _kӓsespӓtzle_ is the epitome of German comfort food. It’s especially popular in [Swabia](https://www.britannica.com/place/Swabia) and Bavaria, where spӓtzle was historically a peasant food due to its simple and inexpensive ingredients. Now, it’s an irresistible treat beloved all around Germany. Think soft egg noodles layered with grated cheese and topped with crispy fried onions — it’s the perfect hearty dish to come home to after a busy day of [sightseeing in Munich](https://www.getyourguide.com/munich-l26/sightseeing-tc2030/).\n\n### How to eat the best kӓsespӓtzle in Germany?\n\nKӓsespӓtzle is served at most German restaurants. Given its filling and indulgent flavors, it’s also popular at festivals and outdoor events, such as Munich’s [Oktoberfest](https://www.getyourguide.com/oktoberfest-l3571/) and Christmas markets across Germany.\n\n## See the sights in Munich\n\n*   [![Image 17: image](https://cdn.getyourguide.com/image/format=auto%2Cfit=cover%2Cgravity=auto%2Cquality=60%2Cwidth=270%2Cheight=180%2Cdpr=2/tour_img/a638081479f3d7d0.jpeg) From Munich: Neuschwanstein & Linderhof Castle Full-Day Trip Discover the UNESCO Heritage sites and explore the castles of King Ludwig II. Explore Neuschwanstein Castle and Linderhof Palace, which are part of the renowned castles of King Ludwig II of Bavaria. 4.6 (17,152) From$85](https://www.getyourguide.com/munich-l26/from-munich-neuschwanstein-linderhof-castle-full-day-trip-t1753/?ranking_uuid=57867fc5-20bb-4a77-93c2-972ae90ba0b1) \n*   [![Image 18: image](https://cdn.getyourguide.com/image/format=auto%2Cfit=cover%2Cgravity=auto%2Cquality=60%2Cwidth=270%2Cheight=180%2Cdpr=2/tour_img/5e21b60fe29de.jpeg) Munich: Third Reich & WWII Walking Tour Germany was emerging from World War I with Munich in the grips of hyperinflation. Out of this fertile soil of chaos rose the Nazi movement and one of history’s most powerful dictators, Adolf Hitler. 4.6 (7,721) From$33](https://www.getyourguide.com/munich-l26/munich-third-reich-wwii-walking-tour-t528/?ranking_uuid=57867fc5-20bb-4a77-93c2-972ae90ba0b1) \n*   [![Image 19: image](https://cdn.getyourguide.com/image/format=auto%2Cfit=cover%2Cgravity=auto%2Cquality=60%2Cwidth=270%2Cheight=180%2Cdpr=2/tour_img/561cee424d1304d07b9e586dbb0b679004b794a41d8f79f715f18f22e63a2160.jpg) Munich: Costumed Night Watchman Tour in English Follow a lantern-carrying night watchman in medieval clothes through Munich's little alleys and hard-to-find sights. Hear stories of medieval city life, from cradle to grave. 4.3 (189) From$21](https://www.getyourguide.com/munich-l26/munich-night-watchman-tour-in-english-t867818/?ranking_uuid=57867fc5-20bb-4a77-93c2-972ae90ba0b1) \n*    \n\n## 5. Schnitzel\n\n![Image 20: A breaded wiener schnitzel served on a wooden board with lemon wedges.](https://cdn.getyourguide.com/image/format=auto%2Cfit=cover%2Cgravity=auto%2Cquality=60/dam/pexels-gonzalo-ruiz-204252201-18275671-LOWRES.jpg)\nExperience the famous wiener schnitzel tradition.\n\nDespite being a staple in German cuisine, the schnitzel likely originated next door in Austria, in Vienna in the 19th century. It’s a simple dish of thin veal or pork coated in flour, egg, and breadcrumbs and fried until golden brown. It’s now a must-eat when tasting your way around Germany.\n\n### How to eat the best schnitzel in Germany?\n\nA classic way to enjoy schnitzel is with a simple squeeze of lemon juice, which adds refreshing acidity that complements the rich, fried meat. Spend the weekend in Cologne, known for its vibrant food scene, and try schnitzel at iconic restaurants such as Bei Oma Kleinmann and Gaffel am Dom.\n\n## Discover Cologne’s foodie experiences\n\n*   [![Image 21: image](https://cdn.getyourguide.com/image/format=auto%2Cfit=cover%2Cgravity=auto%2Cquality=60%2Cwidth=270%2Cheight=180%2Cdpr=2/tour_img/60dee990960a6.jpeg) Cologne: Chocolate Museum Entrance Ticket Visit the Chocolate Museum in Cologne and discover the 5,000-year cultural history of cocoa and chocolate. Sauerbraten\n\n![Image 24: A plate of sauerbraten with red cabbage and potato dumplings.](https://cdn.getyourguide.com/image/format=auto%2Cfit=cover%2Cgravity=auto%2Cquality=60/dam/iStock-533748109-LOWRES.jpg)\nRelish savory sauerbraten, a must-try German dish.\n\nRegarded as one of Germany’s national dishes, _sauerbraten_ is a traditional beef pot roast. The meat is marinated for several days in a mixture of vinegar or wine, and a variety of seasonings before being slow-cooked until tender, giving it a distinctive tangy flavor. Hence its name, which translates to “sour roast.”\n\nIt’s believed to have ancient origins and was likely originally created as a way to preserve meat. While it’s mainly associated with the [Rhineland-Palatinate region](https://www.getyourguide.com/rhineland-palatinate-l601/), it’s also popular in Franconia, Bavaria, and Swabia.\n\n### How to eat the best sauerbraten in Germany?\n\nWhen exploring things to eat in Cologne, don't miss a chance to enjoy sauerbraten with sides like rotkohl (red cabbage) and kartoffelklöße (potato dumplings).\n\n## Find food experiences in Rhineland-Palatinate\n\n*   [![Image 25: image](https://cdn.getyourguide.com/image/format=auto%2Cfit=cover%2Cgravity=auto%2Cquality=60%2Cwidth=270%2Cheight=180%2Cdpr=2/tour_img/305bfa44459680a358608e7a4f09e1c68613411fe295dc692c9b4728a9aee175.jpg) Cochem: Cruise with Lock from Cochem to Beilstein and Back Experience a relaxing boat trip with a lock passage and the best food and drink on board. Enjoy the view of vineyard slopes and Moselle towns with the romantic Beilstein as your destination. 4.5 (569) From$28](https://www.getyourguide.com/beilstein-l177059/cochem-cruise-with-lock-from-cochem-to-beilstein-and-back-t1269229/?ranking_uuid=a181b637-410a-4e5c-b6cb-45578778f653) \n*   [![Image 26: image](https://cdn.getyourguide.com/image/format=auto%2Cfit=cover%2Cgravity=auto%2Cquality=60%2Cwidth=270%2Cheight=180%2Cdpr=2/tour_img/fda65f8440d0414099e7e582f290d127affd1ee1a75acf2a2ad63d2abfc2ebae.jpg) Mainz Winter Magic – Guided City Tour with Mulled Wine & Wine Discover Mainz's Christmas markets, enjoy red and white mulled wine from Stadtweingut Mainz and a glass of red wine from Weingut Braunewell. Currywurst\n\n![Image 28: The exterior of a currywurst shop with red chairs and a neon sign.](https://cdn.getyourguide.com/image/format=auto%2Cfit=cover%2Cgravity=auto%2Cquality=60/dam/pexels-moodsofana-20010968-LOWRES.jpg)\nExplore Berlin’s street food with a legendary currywurst.\n\nLooking for the best food on the go? _Currywurst_ is your answer. This popular German fast food consists of steamed, then fried pork sausage, which is cut into bite-sized pieces and served with a generous topping of curry ketchup. It’s often accompanied by a side of fries or a _brötchen_ (bread roll).\n\nThe [history of currywurst](https://www.nationalgeographic.com/travel/article/what-is-currywurst-where-to-eat-berlin-germany) traces back to post-World War II Berlin. It was invented by Herta Heuwer and sold at a street stand in [Charlottenburg](https://www.getyourguide.com/charlottenburg-l108187/), one of [Berlin’s best neighborhoods](https://www.getyourguide.com/explorer/berlin-ttd17/best-neighbourhoods-in-berlin/). It’s now considered the best food to eat when exploring the city.\n\n### How to eat the best currywurst in Germany?\n\nHead to the Prenzlauer Berg district and try it at Konnopke’s Imbiss, an iconic currywurst stand that has been serving delicious street food since 1930.\n\n## Book a street food tour of Berlin\n\n*   [![Image 29: image](https://cdn.getyourguide.com/image/format=auto%2Cfit=cover%2Cgravity=auto%2Cquality=60%2Cwidth=270%2Cheight=180%2Cdpr=2/tour_img/fec8a283f5b02c5ed5c45dcad48007f22893ece03fe7d9ab58e7ba223eb60b0c.jpg) Berlin: Big Bus Hop-On Hop-Off Tour with Boat Cruise Option Explore Berlin’s top landmarks on an open-top hop-on hop-off bus tour. Rouladen\n\n![Image 32: A plate of rouladen with red cabbage and potato dumplings, garnished with parsley.](https://cdn.getyourguide.com/image/format=auto%2Cfit=cover%2Cgravity=auto%2Cquality=60/dam/iStock-2063456331-LOWRES.jpg)\nSavor hearty rouladen, especially on Sundays and festive occasions.\n\nOne of the most popular foods to try when exploring Germany is _rouladen_, a traditional and unique dish made of thinly sliced beef rolled around a filling of bacon, onions, mustard, and pickles. The rolled beef is browned and braised in a rich gravy until tender. It’s a meal that has long been associated with Sunday dinners and festive occasions in Germany.\n\n### How to eat the best rouladen in Germany?\n\nIt’s popular in Rhineland, Bavaria, and Saxony. In Saxony’s cities [Dresden](https://www.getyourguide.com/dresden-l20/ttd/) and Leipzig, you’ll find rouladen served alongside hearty sides such as potato dumplings and red cabbage.\n\n## Find a food experience in Dresden\n\n*   [![Image 33: image](https://cdn.getyourguide.com/image/format=auto%2Cfit=cover%2Cgravity=auto%2Cquality=60%2Cwidth=270%2Cheight=180%2Cdpr=2/tour_img/637394cd9b12f.jpeg) Dresden: Evening River Cruise with Dinner Enjoy an evening cruise along the Elbe river and admire the views of Dresden’s castles, the Blue Wonder bridge, and the old town. Enjoy a 3-course meal and background music as you take in the sights. 4 (424) From$62](https://www.getyourguide.com/dresden-l20/dresden-winter-lights-evening-river-cruise-with-dinner-t434908/?ranking_uuid=afde1bdc-660a-48cf-8593-360993ecac2c) \n*   [![Image 34: image](https://cdn.getyourguide.com/image/format=auto%2Cfit=cover%2Cgravity=auto%2Cquality=60%2Cwidth=270%2Cheight=180%2Cdpr=2/tour_img/494abcd172a3a6a8a1ab1743d0d14ee3c292e29ac544e653f08c267dbaf2a327.jpg) Dresden: Dresden Beer Safari An enjoyable and entertaining discovery tour through the regional beer culture, with funny anecdotes and five tastings. 4.8 (74) From$24](https://www.getyourguide.com/dresden-l20/dresden-beer-safari-t638156/?ranking_uuid=afde1bdc-660a-48cf-8593-360993ecac2c) \n*   [![Image 35: image](https://cdn.getyourguide.com/image/format=auto%2Cfit=cover%2Cgravity=auto%2Cquality=60%2Cwidth=270%2Cheight=180%2Cdpr=2/tour_img/6479d09d1ed7d.jpeg) Dresden: Private custom tour with a local guide Explore Dresden alongside a local guide! Knödel\n\n![Image 36: A plate with three dumplings,garnished with chives and a flower.](https://cdn.getyourguide.com/image/format=auto%2Cfit=cover%2Cgravity=auto%2Cquality=60/dam/49424124862_89a7e33d77_o-LOWRES.jpg)\n\nphoto: Ritten_Renon\n\nDelight in colorful dumplings, a must-eat treat.\n\nGermany’s version of a dumpling does not disappoint. Larger than your average dumpling and incredibly versatile, _knödel_ are made with a mixture of bread, potatoes, flour, and semolina and boiled until soft and sticky. They can be paired with a variety of dishes, from roast meats to rich gravies and soups. You can even find them as a dessert, topped with fruit compote, cinnamon sugar, or a dollop of whipped cream.\n\n### How to eat the best knödel in Germany?\n\nBavaria is one of the best places in Germany to try traditional knödel, particularly _semmelknödel_ (bread-based) and _kartoffelknödel_ (potato-based). Wash yours down with a local lager — a [brewery tour in Munich](https://www.getyourguide.com/munich-l26/beer-tasting-brewery-tours-tc167/) will help you discover your favorite style.\n\n## Book a food tour in Munich\n\n*   [![Image 37: image](https://cdn.getyourguide.com/image/format=auto%2Cfit=cover%2Cgravity=auto%2Cquality=60%2Cwidth=270%2Cheight=180%2Cdpr=2/tour_img/e076ccf42e47628c39dd6f9d0aee5da15c4eb467a388a6985ae255c07a35f65b.jpg) Munich: Old Town Food Tour with 10+ Tastings, Beer & Pretzel Taste 10+ Bavarian specialties in Munich’s open-air market & historic beerhalls. Lebkuchen\n\n![Image 40: Star-shaped gingerbread cookies decorated with seeds and nuts.](https://cdn.getyourguide.com/image/format=auto%2Cfit=cover%2Cgravity=auto%2Cquality=60/dam/pexels-leefinvrede-29543318-LOWRES.jpg)\nCelebrate with festive German gingerbread cookies.\n\nHeading to the [Christmas markets in Germany](https://www.getyourguide.com/germany-l169009/christmas-activities-tc14/)? Try _lebkuchen_, traditional German gingerbread, which was invented by monks in Franconia in the 13th century. This delicious spiced cookie or cake is made with a blend of Christmas spices, honey or molasses, nuts such as almonds, hazelnuts or walnuts, and candied fruits. It comes in various shapes and sizes and is often decorated with icing, nuts, or chocolate.\n\n### How to eat the best lebkuchen in Germany?\n\n[Nuremberg](https://www.getyourguide.com/nuremberg-l127/ttd/) is famous for its lebkuchen, particularly _elisenlebkuchen_ (made with more nuts than flour). Visit local bakeries, such as Fraunholz Lebkuchen, and the Nuremberg Christmas market to pick some up.\n\n## FAQs\n\n### What is a typical German breakfast?\n\nA typical German breakfast features a variety of brötchen (bread rolls) served with various spreads and toppings. Common choices include marmalade, chocolate spread, honey, and a selection of cheeses and cold cuts like ham and salami.\n\n### What is Germany’s most eaten food?\n\nBratwurst is among Germany’s most famous foods, known for its flavorful sausage made from pork, beef, or veal. Other favorites include sauerbraten, a pot roast, and _wiener schnitzel_, a breaded cutlet.\n\n### What are some popular German meat dishes?\n\nGermany offers a variety of delicious meat dishes. Try _mett_, a ground meat specialty, or _schwenkbraten_, a marinated pork dish from [Saarland](https://www.getyourguide.com/saarland-l96328/). Bavaria is famous for _schweinebraten_, a roasted pork dish. _Jägerschnitzel_ and _rahmschnitzel_ are classic schnitzel variations, with the latter featuring a creamy sauce.\n\n### What are the three main meals in Germany?\n\nIn Germany, breakfast, known as “frühstück,” is usually a light meal. Lunch, or “mittagessen,” is often the main meal of the day, featuring hearty dishes. Dinner, called “abendessen” or “abendbrot,” is typically lighter and may include bread, cold cuts, and cheeses.\n\n### What is a typical German menu?\n\nA typical German menu often features classic dishes such as wiener schnitzel, _würstchen mit sauerkraut_ (sausages and fermented cabbage), and various potato-based options. These might include _bratkartoffeln_ (roasted potatoes) or _kartoffelauflauf_ (potato casseroles).\n\n[![Image 41: influencer-profile-image](https://cdn.getyourguide.com/image/format=auto%2Cfit=crop%2Cgravity=auto%2Cquality=60%2Cwidth=100%2Cheight=100/dam/Kiawna%20Brewster_crop.png)](https://www.getyourguide.com/explorer/creators/kiawna-brewster/)\n\nAbout the author\n\nKiawna is a California-raised, Berlin-based travel writer, content creator, and teacher. These might include _bratkartoffeln_ (roasted potatoes) or _kartoffelauflauf_ (potato casseroles).\n\n## Read more about Germany\n\n*   [![Image 46: 10 best national parks in Germany for the ultimate escape](https://cdn.getyourguide.com/image/format=auto%2Cfit=crop%2Cgravity=auto%2Cquality=60%2Cwidth=425%2Cheight=240/dam/konigssee-2399711-%20Header%20Desktop.jpg) ### 10 best national parks in Germany for the ultimate escape Germans often say, “There’s no such thing as bad weather, just inadequate clothing.” So, be prepared when visiting the best national parks in Germany — from Western Pomerania’s sandy beaches to the Black Forest’s dense woodlands and the alpine landscapes of Berchtesgaden.The best time to visit Germany for its national parks depends on your interests: it could be hiking in Bavaria in summer, climbing in Saxon Switzerland in fall, or birdwatching along the Wadden Sea during migration seasons. Whether you’re an intrepid explorer or traveling with kids, here are the best German national parks for a scenic outdoor adventure.Ashley Fischer • Jun 23, 2026](https://www.getyourguide.com/explorer/germany-ttd169009/national-parks-in-germany/)   \n*   [![Image 47: 9 best places to visit in Germany you don’t want to miss](https://cdn.getyourguide.com/image/format=auto%2Cfit=crop%2Cgravity=auto%2Cquality=60%2Cwidth=425%2Cheight=240/dam/tim-dennert-diomzake9Eg-unsplash-Header%20Desktop.jpg) ### 9 best places to visit in Germany you don’t want to miss With the country’s diverse landscape and rich history, putting together a list of the best places to visit in Germany can seem impossible. From Berlin’s iconic landmarks to imposing castles to the awe-inspiring vistas and total serenity of the Black Forest, this titan of Central Europe captivates with its storied past and stunning natural beauty.
